Montgomery Township (Somerset County)                    (STATEWIDE)
DEP TO HOLD PUBLIC MEETING JULY 25                       NO. 88/14.
ON MONTGOMERY TWP. HOUSING DEVELOPMENT SITE               Immediate release:
                                                         July 16. 1987

    TRENTON—A public meeting will be held Wednesday, July 29, on the completion of an
Interim Remedial Investigation/Feasibility Study (RI/FS) for private potable wells at the
Montgomery Township Housing Development Superfund site 1n Somerset County, the state
Department of Environmental Protection (DEP) has announced.
     A comprehensive RI/FS that encompasses an approximate two square miles In Montgomery
Township and Rocky Hill Borough was awarded previously at a cost of more than $950,000
to Woodward-Clyde Consultants of Wayne. That RI/FS will Investigate the source of
trichloroethylene (TCE) and other contaminants 1n the two municipalities and 1s expected
to be completed by the end of this year or early In 1988.
    The RI/FS \contract was amended earlier this year, however, to provide an Interim
report that exclusively addresses the drinking water supply for residents of the
Montgomery Township Housing Development. The Interim report was contracted for at a
cost of approximately $42,000.
     The site 1s ranked 65th of 100 New Jersey sites Included or proposed for Inclusion
on the National Priorities List (Superfund).
     The wells, which have been found to be contaminated or threatened by contamination
                                                                    •
with TCE and tetrachloroethylene. are on the properties of 77 homes on Sycamore Lane.
                                                                                            G>
Robin Drive, Oxford Circle, Cleveland Circle and Montgomery Road.
                                                                                            O
     While TCE contamination originally force the closing of the Rocky Kill Borough         O
                                                                                   *
municipal well, a treatment system has been In j>lace In that municipality since July
of 1983.

      Add 1
  *           •
                  In Montgomery Township, 39 of the total 77 resfdences whose wells are
 ontaminated or threatened by contamination have not hooked up to a public water
supply.                                                                          -
        The RI/FS for the private Montgomery potable wells evaluated remedial alternatives.
Including no action, Individual well water treatment, provisions for temporary drinking
water supplies, a new centralized community well with treatment, and hookups to an
Ellzabethtown Water Company line.
        The alternative recommended by the DEP and the United States Environmental Protection
Agency (EPA) Is residential hookups to the Ellzabethtown Water Company line. This
alternative will effectively eliminate the possibility of residential exposure to ground-
water contamination in a cost-effective manner.
        Ellzabethtown Water Company already has water mains available on Sycamore Lane,
Robin Drive, Oxford Circle, Cleveland Circle and a portion of Montgomery Road.
       The federal government would pay for any future hookups or water line extensions
that would be necessary under this recommendation.
        The public meeting on the recommended alternative will be held Wednesday, July 29,
at 7:30 p.m. in the Montgomery Township Municipal Building, 2261 Route 206, In Montgomery
Township.

                                                       (STATEWIDE}
                                                      NO. 88/136
          , ROCKY HILL REMEDIATION FLANS              Immediate release:
TO BE REVIEWED AT MAY 12 PUBLIC MEETING               April 22, 1988

           TRENTON—Seven alternative remediation proposals for Superfund sites
in Montgomery Township and Rocky Hill Borough, Somerset County, will be
                                   •

discussed at a public meeting May 12 at 7 p.m. in the Montgomery Township
Municipal Building, 2261 Rt. 206. The meeting sponsored by the federal
Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) and state Department of Environmental
Protection    (DEP) is      to   discuss        the   results   of   the   Remedial
Investigation/Feasibility Study (RI/FS) and to receive public carments.
           Camissioner Richard T. Dewling of the DEP said the sites are the
Montgomery Township Housing Development and the Rocky Hill Municipal Wellfield.
       \

In both cases ground water contamination with trichloroethene (TCE) is the
problem.
           A single RI/FS was performed for both sites by Woodward-Clyde
Consultants of Wayne at a cost of $990,000 because of their close proximity,
Dewling said. He added that, copies of the proposed remedial action plan and
RI/FS documents are available for examination at five locations.
           The alternative favored by DEP and EPA, "is the most appropriate site
remedy, effectively protecting public health and the environment in the most
cost-effective and direct way, while best meeting all appropriate regulations,"
                                                                                      o
Dewling said.    This alternative calls for extracting and purifying ground           o
water, after which it would be reinjected into the ground; sealing affected
                                              /
                                       (more)

===== SOURCE PDF FILE PAGE 200 (file index only; use the page number printed in the source text for evidence anchors) =====

Add 1

private wells; and connecting one residence in the southwest section of the
site to public vater.                                         __
                                                              «•
         The veils in Montgomery Township affected by or threatened with TCE
are on properties of 77 hones on Sycamore lane, Robin Drive, Oxford Circle,
Cleveland Circle and Montgomery Road. The sealing of many of these wells has
been addressed separately in a decision issued by EPA and DEP in September 1987
which also includes hookups of the residences to a public supply.
         The Rocky Hill municipal well, which had been shut down due to TCE
contamination, went back into service in 1983 after installation of a water
purification system which is still in operation, Dewling observed.
          The proposed remedial action plan and study documents are available
for public review at these locations:

Add 2


           Both written and oral comments may be presented at the May 12
meeting.    In addition, written comments may be submitted untilTthe close of
business on May 23, 1988. Comments should be submitted to:
                         Grace L. Singer, Chief
                         Bureau of Community Relations
                         Division of Hazardous Site Mitigation
                         Department of Envirormental Protection
 -                       401 E. State Street - 6th Floor
 ~"                      CN 413
                         Trenton, N.J. 08625
           Further information on the plan may be obtained by writing to the
above address or by calling Jeffrey Folmer, Connunity Relations Coordinator, at
(609) 984-3081.
           Brief descriptions of the seven evaluated remediation alternative
follow:
Alternative 1; No Action
No activities would be carried out to provide control measures to mitigate the
contamination or isolate the public from the contaminated ground water.
Contaminant levels and distribution would be monitored on a regular basis to
observe possible changes that may warrant additional measures.
Alternative 2: Aquifer Isolation
This alternative requires that present private wells be sealed and future well
installations be restricted in order to isolate the aquifer from uncontrolled
potable water usage. In addition, residences within the contamination plume,
including one additional residence not previously identified, would be provided
with public water. The monitoring elements from Alternative 1 would also be
performed.
Alternative 3: Pump and Air Strip from the Bitire Plume
In addition to the water connection and well sealing component identified in
Alternative 2, this alternative employs an aquifer flushing system using
approximately 13 extraction wells. The alternative is further divided into
three sub-alternatives, which differ in the possible discharge locations for
the treated ground water (3A-oconomical combination of surface water and uround
water discharge} 3B-groundwater injection; 3C-use as a potable water supply). ~
Alternative 4: Pump and Treat with Carbon Adsorption from the Entire Plume
This alternative is identical to Alternative 3 and its sub-alternatives except
that activated carbon adsorption is the central unit process.              ..     ^
Alternative 5: Pump and Air Strip from the Source Area                            ^
This alternative includes the water connection' and well sealing components of    Q
Alternative 2 and an aquifer flushing system using approximately three            o
extraction wells located in the most contaminated areas of the site. The          ^
alternative is further divided into three sub-alternatives, which differ in the
                                                                                  "Xl
                                                                                  Nl

                                  (more)

===== SOURCE PDF FILE PAGE 202 (file index only; use the page number printed in the source text for evidence anchors) =====

Add 3                                                                                 \


possible discharge locations for the treated ground water (5A-ground water
injection downgradient; SB-ground water injection upgradient; 5C use as a
potable water supply).                                       _r-
Alternative 6; Punp and Treat with Carbon Adsorption from the Source Area
This alternative is identical to Alternative 5 and its. sub-alternatives,
except that carbon adsorption is the central unit process.
AltexTiative 7; 'Pu,p and Air Stripping of Source Area with Vicinity Injection
This alternative is a variation of Alternative 5B. It uses the same three
source area extraction wells outlined in Alternative 5 and 6 however, instead
of incorporating a single centralized treatment unit, individual air strippers
are installed at each punping location. Discharge is from each unit to two
injection uells located at nearby points selected to enhance flushing.
